1: # Role-Based Admin Panel Authentication and Access Control
2: 
3: This plan details the implementation of Step 1 (Role seeding), Step 2 (User creation), Step 3 (Bcrypt-backed user login), and Step 4 (Role-based access control and page visibility).
4: 
5: ---
6: 
7: ## 1. Database Model Alignment
8: 
9: The database schema defined in pgAdmin contains `roles` and `users` tables, but the Python backend SQLAlchemy models do not reflect these relationships. We will update the models in `backend/app/models/__init__.py`.
10: 
11: ### [NEW] `Role` model
12: ```python
13: class Role(Base):
14:     __tablename__ = "roles"
15:     role_id = Column(Integer, primary_key=True, index=True)
16:     role_name = Column(String(50), unique=True, nullable=False)
17:     description = Column(Text, nullable=True)
18: ```
19: 
20: ### [MODIFY] `User` model
21: We will update `class User` to match the PostgreSQL schema:
22: ```python
23: class User(Base):
24:     __tablename__ = "users"
25:     user_id = Column(Uuid, primary_key=True, default=uuid.uuid4)
26:     username = Column(String(100), unique=True, nullable=False)
27:     email = Column(String(150), unique=True, nullable=True)
28:     password_hash = Column(Text, nullable=False)
29:     full_name = Column(String(150), nullable=True)
30:     role_id = Column(Integer, ForeignKey("roles.role_id"), nullable=True)
31:     district_id = Column(Uuid, ForeignKey("districts.district_id"), nullable=True)
32:     is_active = Column(Boolean, default=True)
33:     last_login = Column(DateTime(timezone=False), nullable=True)
34:     created_at = Column(
35:         DateTime(timezone=False),
36:         server_default=func.now()
37:     )
38: ```
39: 
40: ---
41: 
42: ## 2. Step 1 — Seeding Roles and default Super Admin
43: 
44: At startup in `backend/main.py` (inside `startup()`), we will check if the `roles` table is empty and seed exactly:
45: 1. `super_admin`
46: 2. `admin`
47: 3. `auditor`
48: 4. `viewer`
49: 
50: If the `users` table is empty, we will create a default `super_admin` user using:
51: - **Username**: `admin`
52: - **Email**: `admin@hcverify.local`
53: - **Password**: `adminpassword` (hashed using bcrypt)
54: 
55: ---
56: 
57: ## 3. Step 2 & 3 — Bcrypt Hashing, Login, and User Creation Endpoints
58: 
59: We will use the newly installed python `bcrypt` package to securely hash and verify passwords.
60: 
61: ### Security Utility Updates
62: In `backend/app/utils/security.py`, we will add helper methods:
63: ```python
64: import bcrypt
65: 
66: def hash_password_bcrypt(password: str) -> str:
67:     salt = bcrypt.gensalt()
68:     return bcrypt.hashpw(password.encode("utf-8"), salt).decode("utf-8")
69: 
70: def verify_password_bcrypt(plain_password: str, hashed_password: str) -> bool:
71:     return bcrypt.checkpw(plain_password.encode("utf-8"), hashed_password.encode("utf-8"))
72: ```
73: 
74: ### Auth & User Endpoints
75: We will modify `/admin/login` and add `/admin/users/` routes to:
76: 1. **Login**: Look up user by email, verify `is_active == True`, match password with bcrypt, and return a JWT containing `user_id` and `role_name`.
77: 2. **Access Protection**: Add a dependency `get_current_admin_user` that extracts the JWT and validates the user role.
78: 3. **User Management**:
79:    - `GET /admin/users/` (super_admin only).
80:    - `POST /admin/users/` (super_admin only; handles creating new users, hashing passwords, and dropdown roles).
81:    - `DELETE /admin/users/{userId}` (super_admin only).
82: 
83: ---
84: 
85: ## 4. Step 4 — Frontend and Backend Access Control
86: 
87: ### Backend Route Checks
88: We will modify standard endpoints in `backend/main.py` to enforce the following roles:
89: - **`super_admin`**: Full access to all endpoints.
90: - **`admin`**: Access to elections, candidates, districts, settings, and voting status checks. No access to user management `/admin/users/`.
91: - **`auditor`**: Read-only access to `/admin/audit`, `/admin/suspicious-activity`, `/admin/election-security` (sync logs/blockchain blocks). Write/POST endpoints return a 403.
92: - **`viewer`**: Read-only access to public announcements and results only. All other endpoints return a 403.
93: 
94: ### Frontend Rendering Changes
95: In `frontend/src/pages/AdminPage.jsx` and `frontend/src/pages/AdminLogin.jsx`:
96: 1. **AdminLogin.jsx**:
97:    - Update form labels to request "Email" instead of "Username".
98:    - Submit `{ email, password }` in the POST request payload.
99: 2. **AdminPage.jsx**:
100:    - Decode the JWT token to extract the logged-in user's `role_name`.
101:    - Update `navigationGroups` dynamically based on the role so tabs that are unauthorized are completely omitted from the menu and rendering.
102:    - Hide/disable Action buttons (like Create User, New District, resolves, manual votes, deletes) for `auditor` and `viewer` roles.
103: 
104: ---
105: 
106: ## 5. Verification Plan
107: 
108: ### Automated Tests
109: We will write a new regression test script `backend/test_role_access.py` to verify:
110: 1. Role-based login works and returns a correct JWT.
111: 2. An auditor or viewer gets a `403 Forbidden` on writing endpoints.
112: 3. A non-super_admin gets `403 Forbidden` on user creation.
113: 
114: ### Manual Verification
115: 1. Log in with the default seeded super admin user (`admin@hcverify.local` / `adminpassword`).
116: 2. Create a new user with the `auditor` role.
117: 3. Log out and log back in as the auditor.
118: 4. Verify that only authorized tabs (`Dashboard`, `Blockchain`, `Security`, `Election Security`) are visible and that you cannot perform write actions.
119:

2: 
3: We have fully implemented role-based user creation, login, and access control for the admin panel.

5: ---
6: 
7: ## 🛠️ Summary of Changes
8: 
9: ### 1. Database Model Alignment
10: - Defined `Role` model and aligned `User` model with the exact columns and relations specified in the PostgreSQL database schema inside `backend/app/models/__init__.py`.
11: 
12: ### 2. Bcrypt Hashing Integration
13: - Added `hash_password_bcrypt` and `verify_password_bcrypt` helper functions using the `bcrypt` library inside `backend/app/utils/security.py`.
14: 
15: ### 3. Role Seeding & Default Super Admin
16: - Configured backend startup (`backend/main.py`) to automatically seed exactly four roles (`super_admin`, `admin`, `auditor`, `viewer`) into the database if empty.
17: - If the users table is empty, a default super admin user is created using the credentials configured in the environment variables:
18:   - **Email**: `admin@hcverify.local`
19:   - **Password**: `adminpassword` (hashed with bcrypt)
20: 
21: ### 4. Backend Endpoints & Access Control
22: - Rewrote `POST /admin/login` to authenticate against database-backed users with bcrypt and return a JWT containing `user_id` and `role_name`.
23: - Implemented `/admin/users/` (GET, POST, DELETE) user management endpoints restricted strictly to `super_admin`.
24: - Restricted all standard admin read and write endpoints globally or handler-level using `enforce_role` (e.g. read-only roles cannot invoke write operations).
25: 
26: ### 5. Frontend Navigation & Protection
27: - Updated `AdminLogin.jsx` to take **Email** and submit `{ email, password }` to `/admin/login`.
28: - Updated `AdminPage.jsx` to parse the `role_name` from the JWT and dynamically filter `navigationGroups`. Tabs like `Users`, `Districts`, `Elections`, `Candidates`, `Voters`, `Votes`, `Settings` are completely omitted from rendering for unauthorized roles (like `auditor` or `viewer`).
29: - Added window-level double confirmation check in `AdminPage.jsx` before creating a user with the `super_admin` role.
30: - Conditionalized mount queries so that 403 errors are not triggered on the dashboard for restricted endpoints.

1: import hashlib
2: import uuid
3: import logging
4: from datetime import datetime
5: from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession
6: from sqlalchemy.future import select
7: from sqlalchemy import desc
8: from app.models import DistrictSyncLog, BlockchainTransaction
9: 
10: logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
11: 
12: SIMULATED_DISTRICTS = ["District A", "District B", "District C", "District D"]
13: 
14: def _hash_sync(district: str, block_index: int, block_hash: str, timestamp: datetime) -> str:
15:     t_str = timestamp.astimezone().replace(tzinfo=None).isoformat() if timestamp.tzinfo else timestamp.isoformat()
16:     payload = f"{district}|{block_index}|{block_hash}|{t_str}"
17:     return hashlib.sha256(payload.encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()
18: 
19: async def sync_block(db: AsyncSession, block: BlockchainTransaction):
20:     try:
21:         timestamp = datetime.utcnow()
22:         for district in SIMULATED_DISTRICTS:
23:             sync_hash = _hash_sync(district, block.block_index, block.current_block_hash, timestamp)
24:             
25:             sync_log = DistrictSyncLog(
26:                 district_name=district,
27:                 blockchain_transaction_id=block.id,
28:                 block_index=block.block_index,
29:                 receipt_id=block.receipt_id,
30:                 sync_timestamp=timestamp,
31:                 sync_hash=sync_hash,
32:                 sync_status="Synced"
33:             )
34:             db.add(sync_log)
35:             
36:         await db.commit()
37:     except Exception as e:
38:         logger.error(f"Failed to synchronize block {block.block_index} to districts: {e}")
39:         # Rule 8: Sync failures never affect votes/receipts/hashes/blockchain
40:         return False
41:     return True
42: 
43: async def verify_synchronization(db: AsyncSession):
44:     # Fetch all sync logs ordered by block index and district
45:     result = await db.execute(select(DistrictSyncLog).order_by(DistrictSyncLog.block_index, DistrictSyncLog.district_name))
46:     logs = result.scalars().all()
47:     
48:     if not logs:
49:         return "SYNCED"
50:         
51:     # Group logs by block_index
52:     from collections import defaultdict
53:     block_logs = defaultdict(list)
54:     for log in logs:
55:         block_logs[log.block_index].append(log)
56:         
57:     for index, d_logs in block_logs.items():
58:         # Check if all simulated districts received the block
59:         if len(d_logs) != len(SIMULATED_DISTRICTS):
60:             return "OUT_OF_SYNC"
61:             
62:         # Verify block hashes are identical across all districts
63:         expected_blockchain_id = d_logs[0].blockchain_transaction_id
64:         for log in d_logs:
65:             if log.blockchain_transaction_id != expected_blockchain_id:
66:                 return "OUT_OF_SYNC"
67:                 
68:             # Fetch actual block to recompute sync hash
69:             block_result = await db.execute(select(BlockchainTransaction).where(BlockchainTransaction.id == log.blockchain_transaction_id))
70:             block = block_result.scalars().first()
71:             if not block:
72:                 return "OUT_OF_SYNC"
73:                 
74:             expected_hash = _hash_sync(log.district_name, log.block_index, block.current_block_hash, log.sync_timestamp)
75:             if expected_hash != log.sync_hash:
76:                 return "OUT_OF_SYNC"
77:                 
78:     return "SYNCED"
79: 
80: # FYP Helper Methods
81: async def get_all_sync_logs(db: AsyncSession):
82:     result = await db.execute(select(DistrictSyncLog).order_by(DistrictSyncLog.created_at))
83:     return result.scalars().all()
84: 
85: async def get_sync_by_district(db: AsyncSession, district: str):
86:     result = await db.execute(select(DistrictSyncLog).where(DistrictSyncLog.district_name == district).order_by(DistrictSyncLog.block_index))
87:     return result.scalars().all()
88: 
89: async def get_sync_by_block(db: AsyncSession, block_index: int):
90:     result = await db.execute(select(DistrictSyncLog).where(DistrictSyncLog.block_index == block_index))
91:     return result.scalars().all()

48:     # Prevent duplicates
49:     existing = await db.execute(
50:         select(District).where(District.district_name == payload.district_name)
51:     )

55:         district_id=uuid.uuid4(),
56:         district_name=payload.district_name,
57:     )
58:     db.add(new_district)
59:     await db.commit()
60:     await db.refresh(new_district)
61:     return new_district

334:     setting_id_int = Column('setting_id', Integer, primary_key=True, index=True)
335: 
336:     @hybrid_property
337:     def setting_id(self):
338:         if self.setting_id_int is not None:
339:             # We pad to a 128-bit integer for a valid UUID
340:             return uuid.UUID(int=self.setting_id_int)
341:         return None
342: 
343:     @setting_id.setter
344:     def setting_id(self, value):
345:         if isinstance(value, uuid.UUID):
346:             self.setting_id_int = value.int & 0xFFFFFFFF
347:         elif isinstance(value, int):
348:             self.setting_id_int = value
349:         elif isinstance(value, str):
350:             try:
351:                 self.setting_id_int = uuid.UUID(value).int & 0xFFFFFFFF
352:             except ValueError:
353:                 self.setting_id_int = int(value)
354: 
355:     @setting_id.expression
356:     def setting_id(cls):
357:         return cls.setting_id_int

36: async def get_district(district_id: uuid.UUID, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db)):
37:     """Return a single district by UUID."""
38:     result = await db.execute(select(District).where(District.district_id == district_id))
39:     district = result.scalars().first()
40:     if not district:
41:         raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="District not found")
42:     return district
43: 
44: 
45: @router.post("/", response_model=DistrictResponse, status_code=201)
46: async def create_district(payload: DistrictCreate, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db)):
47:     """Create a new district."""
48:     # Prevent duplicates
49:     existing = await db.execute(
50:         select(District).where(District.district_name == payload.district_name)
51:     )
52:     if existing.scalars().first():
53:         raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="District with this name already exists.")
54:     new_district = District(
55:         district_id=uuid.uuid4(),
56:         district_name=payload.district_name,
57:     )
58:     db.add(new_district)
59:     await db.commit()
60:     await db.refresh(new_district)
61:     return new_district
62: 
63: 
64: @router.delete("/{district_id}", status_code=204)
65: async def delete_district(district_id: uuid.UUID, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db)):
66:     """Delete a district by UUID."""
67:     result = await db.execute(select(District).where(District.district_id == district_id))
68:     district = result.scalars().first()
69:     if not district:
70:         raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="District not found")
71:     await db.delete(district)
72:     await db.commit()

59:         )
60:     except JWTError:
61:         raise HTTPException(

63:             detail="Invalid token"
64:         )
